The function reset_to_initial_eeg_state is defined in core/snapshot/eeg_session_snapshot.py at line 202. According to [SN01] and [S01], it restores the visualizer to its startup EEG state by clearing active EEG data, plot artists, timeline state, amplitude-map elements, and tab-local controls. It expects a visualizer object that contains a _eeg_snapshot attribute. The function body, as shown in [SN01], is:

def reset_to_initial_eeg_state(visualizer):
 """
 Restore the visualizer to its startup EEG state.

 This clears active EEG data, plot artists, timeline state, amplitude-map
 elements, and tab-local controls that should disappear once an EEG is
 closed.
 """
 if not hasattr(visualizer, "_eeg_snapshot"):
 logger.warning("No EEG snapshot available to restore")
 return

 logger.info("Resetting EEG to initial snapshot")

 _cleanup_amplitude_map(visualizer)
 _reset_amplitude_map_button(visualizer)
 cleanup_all_artists(visualizer)
 _remove_ruler_axis(visualizer)

 if hasattr(visualizer, "ax"):
 _clear_axis_y_decorations(getattr(visualizer, "ax", None), "main axis")

 if hasattr(visualizer, "label_ax"):
 _clear_axis_y_decorations(
 getattr(visualizer, "label_ax", None),
 "label axis",
 )

 if hasattr(visualizer, "ax") and visualizer.ax is not None:
 log_tick_labels(visualizer.ax, "main_ax [after cleanup]")

Pass 1:

Yes — there’s a lot of duplicated instruction text in the current method, especially in the chain/flow branch and the evidence-calibration wording. The version you pasted also keeps code-excerpt requirements in the non-prefer_code chain branch, which is probably not what you want.

Replace the whole method with this deduplicated version:

def _build_answer_style_instructions(question: str, prefer_code: bool) -> str:
    calibration = (
        "Use evidence-calibrated wording such as 'the retrieved snippets show', "
        "'appears to be orchestrated by', 'is delegated to', 'is supported by', "
        "or 'seems to' when the relationship is inferred.\n"
        "Use stronger wording only when the link is directly shown by evidence.\n"
        "Do not present inferred transitions as certain facts unless explicitly shown.\n"
    )

    code_excerpt_rules = (
        "You must include real code excerpts when relevant SOURCE SNIPPETS exist.\n"
        "When code is shown, copy it only from SOURCE SNIPPETS.\n"
        "Do not invent code, reconstruct missing code, or synthesize pseudo-code.\n"
        "Show at least 2 short real code excerpts when the evidence pack contains them.\n"
    )

    chain_evidence_rules = (
        "Every reported step in the flow must include at least one concrete evidence anchor.\n"
        "If a step has no SOURCE SNIPPET, mark it explicitly as 'file-level evidence only' "
        "and do not present it as equally strong as snippet-backed steps.\n"
        "Do not reuse the previous step's anchor for a different symbol.\n"
        "For each handoff step, prefer one short snippet showing either the call site or the callee body.\n"
        "If the middle handoff method is named but its body is not shown, say: "
        "'The call is evidenced, but the callee body is not present in SOURCE SNIPPETS.'\n"
        "If one transition is not directly shown, say exactly which link is missing.\n"
    )

    if _is_code_localized_explanation_question(question):
        base = (
            "ANSWER STYLE\n"
            "For this question, explain the flow or implementation as a code-localized technical walkthrough.\n"
            "Use this order when supported by evidence:\n"
            "1) brief purpose of the flow,\n"
            "2) entry file and entry symbol,\n"
            "3) step-by-step handoff across files or symbols,\n"
        )

        if prefer_code:
            return (
                base
                + "4) real code excerpts from SOURCE SNIPPETS,\n"
                + "5) short note about the missing link if evidence is partial.\n"
                + "Prefer exact evidence-backed file paths, symbol names, snippet anchors, and line numbers when present.\n"
                + code_excerpt_rules
                + "For each excerpt, immediately explain:\n"
                + "- what file it comes from,\n"
                + "- what symbol or anchor it belongs to,\n"
                + "- why it matters in the flow.\n"
                + "Prefer prose plus code localization over prose-only explanation.\n"
                + "When supported by evidence, mention concrete file paths and approximate line anchors from the snippet headers.\n"
                + calibration
            )

        return (
            base
            + "4) code-localization notes using file paths, symbols, and snippet anchors,\n"
            + "5) short note about the missing link if evidence is partial.\n"
            + "Prefer exact evidence-backed file paths, symbol names, snippet anchors, and line numbers when present.\n"
            + "Do not output code blocks unless the user explicitly asks for code.\n"
            + "Focus on precise code localization in prose.\n"
            + calibration
        )

    if _is_explain_implementation_question(question):
        base = (
            "ANSWER STYLE\n"
            "For this question, explain the implementation as a technical walkthrough in plain prose.\n"
            "Use this order when supported by evidence:\n"
            "1) implementation entry point,\n"
            "2) main orchestration class or method,\n"
            "3) rendering or execution class or method,\n"
            "4) update, interaction, or refresh flow,\n"
            "5) one missing link sentence at the end if evidence is partial.\n"
            "Prefer exact evidence-backed symbol names when they are present in the evidence pack.\n"
        )

        if prefer_code:
            return (
                base
                + "If retrieved snippets contain real implementation code, you must include real code excerpts in the answer.\n"
                + "When code is shown, it must be copied only from the provided SOURCE SNIPPETS.\n"
                + "Do not invent code, do not reconstruct missing code, and do not synthesize pseudo-source from description alone.\n"
                + "Show at least 2 short real code excerpts when the evidence pack contains relevant snippets.\n"
                + "For each code excerpt, add a short prose explanation immediately below it.\n"
                + "Prefer code-backed explanation over prose-only explanation in this mode.\n"
                + calibration
            )

        return (
            base
            + "Prefer architectural prose over code excerpts.\n"
            + "Do not output code blocks unless the user explicitly asks for code.\n"
            + "Summarize the implementation mainly in prose, using concrete evidence-backed symbols and relationships.\n"
            + calibration
        )

    if _is_chain_or_flow_question(question):
        base = (
            "ANSWER STYLE\n"
            "For this question, explain the flow step by step in execution order.\n"
            "For reset/cleanup questions, prefer this order when supported by evidence:\n"
            "1) entry/orchestrator symbol,\n"
            "2) direct handoff calls,\n"
            "3) leaf cleanup/reset methods,\n"
            "4) one explicit missing-link note if the invocation path is not shown.\n"
            "Do not lead with leaf cleanup methods if an orchestrator or reset-flow controller is present in evidence.\n"
            "Name the concrete file paths, symbols, and handoff points when supported by evidence.\n"
        )

        if prefer_code:
            return (
                base
                + "If SOURCE SNIPPETS are present, include at least 2 short verbatim code excerpts copied exactly from those snippets to anchor the key handoff points.\n"
                + "For each code excerpt, immediately state the file, symbol, and why it matters in the flow.\n"
                + chain_evidence_rules
                + "Calibrate certainty carefully:\n"
                + "- Use 'the evidence shows' for directly supported transitions.\n"
                + "- Use 'appears to connect to' or 'is likely followed by' only when inferred.\n"
            )

        return (
            base
            + "Do not output code blocks unless the user explicitly asks for code.\n"
            + "Explain the same flow in prose, but still name the concrete file paths, symbols, and handoff points when supported by evidence.\n"
            + chain_evidence_rules
            + "Calibrate certainty carefully:\n"
            + "- Use 'the evidence shows' for directly supported transitions.\n"
            + "- Use 'appears to connect to' or 'is likely followed by' only when inferred.\n"
        )

    if prefer_code:
        return (
            "ANSWER STYLE\n"
            "Answer with a mix of concise prose and short verbatim code excerpts from SOURCE SNIPPETS.\n"
            "Lead with the most evidence-backed file and symbol.\n"
            "Include at least one code excerpt copied verbatim from SOURCE SNIPPETS when available.\n"
            "Do not invent code. If no snippet covers a detail, describe it in prose.\n"
            "If evidence is partial, state the missing link in one sentence at the end.\n"
            "Calibrate certainty carefully and avoid overstating inferred links.\n"
        )

    return (
        "ANSWER STYLE\n"
        "Answer entirely in plain prose. Do NOT output any code blocks.\n"
        "Prefer concrete evidence-backed files, symbols, and relationships over general summary language.\n"
        "Describe what methods do, what they call, and how they connect in English sentences only.\n"
        "If evidence is partial, state the missing link in one sentence at the end.\n"
        "Calibrate certainty carefully and avoid overstating inferred links.\n"
    )

What this fixes:

    removes repeated calibration text across branches

    removes repeated chain-flow wording

    keeps the new “strict evidence per step” rule

    fixes the non-prefer_code chain branch so it no longer asks for code excerpts

    restores a real fallback return path instead of leaving only commented fallback code

Run the same GUI question again after this replacement and paste the result back.

INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE

The local model produced content that does not appear to be fully grounded in the supplied evidence pack. Rejected by rule: forbidden_symbols

That error means the model answered, but the bridge rejected it after generation. I’m checking the validator rule against your current prompt wording so I can tell you exactly what to change next.

I found the new part: your current bridge still decides whether to enforce forbidden_symbols using its own narrow _is_code_localized_prompt(). That means a stricter prompt-builder can make the model mention more code, but the bridge can still reject it afterward.

This is not a new retrieval failure. It is a post-generation bridge rejection again.

Why it happened:

    in the current v10_ai_bridge.py, the generative branch only skips forbidden_symbols when _is_code_localized_prompt(prompt) returns true.

    your current _is_code_localized_prompt(...) is still narrower than the questions you are now testing. Its explanation terms do not include implemented or what code shows, and its code terms do not include phrases like strict evidence, exact snippet, or exact snippets.

So the answer can be good, but the bridge still says “this is not a code-localized prompt,” and then forbidden_symbols stays active and erases it.

Use this exact replacement in v10_ai_bridge.py:

def _is_code_localized_prompt(self, prompt: str) -> bool:
    q = self._extract_user_question(prompt)

    explanation_terms = [
        "explain",
        "trace",
        "walk through",
        "describe",
        "summarize",
        "implementation",
        "implemented",
        "flow",
        "chain",
        "what code shows",
    ]

    code_terms = [
        "with code",
        "show code",
        "include code",
        "code localization",
        "code location",
        "where in code",
        "show snippets",
        "show snippet",
        "snippet",
        "snippets",
        "exact snippet",
        "exact snippets",
        "verbatim code",
        "line numbers",
        "line-level evidence",
        "line-level snippet",
        "line-level snippets",
        "line level snippet",
        "line level snippets",
        "file path and code",
        "symbol and code",
        "code evidence",
        "with code evidence",
        "strict evidence",
        "what code shows",
    ]

    return any(term in q for term in explanation_terms) and any(term in q for term in code_terms)

Why this is the right next move:

    it matches the exact phrasing family you just used

    it uses the bridge you actually have now

    it explains why forbidden_symbols reappeared after your prompt-builder improvement: the model got better instructions, but the bridge classifier stayed narrower.

Then retest the same question that just failed.

If it still fails with forbidden_symbols after this exact replacement, then the next step is stronger and different:
skip forbidden_symbols for all generative snippet-backed prompts, not just code-localized ones. That would be a bridge-policy change, not another phrase tweak. Your session notes already identified forbidden_symbols as the most common false positive and the main source of “answer appears then gets erased.”

    def _is_generative_prompt(self, prompt: str) -> bool:
        q = self._extract_user_question(prompt)

        if self._is_locator_plus_explanation_prompt(prompt):
            return True

        if self._is_deterministic_prompt(prompt):
            return False

        explanation_terms = [
            "explain",
            "summarize",
            "describe",
            "walk through",
            "architecture",
            "responsibility split",
            "implementation of",
        ]

        flow_terms = [
            "trace the chain",
            "trace the flow",
            "explain the chain",
            "startup chain",
            "execution chain",
            "call chain",
            "flow from",
            "sequence from",
            "timeline chain",
            "reset chain",
            "cleanup chain",
            "reset flow",
            "cleanup flow",
        ]

        code_localization_terms = [
            "with code",
            "show code",
            "include code",
            "code localization",
            "code location",
            "where in code",
            "show snippets",
            "show snippet",
            "line numbers",
            "file path and code",
            "symbol and code",
            "code evidence",
            "with code evidence",
            "line-level snippet",
            "line-level snippets",
            "line level snippet",
            "line level snippets",
        ]

        has_explanation_intent = any(term in q for term in explanation_terms)
        has_flow_intent = any(term in q for term in flow_terms)
        import re as _re

        if _re.search(r"\btrace the .+ flow\b", q):
            has_flow_intent = True
        if _re.search(r"\bdescribe the .+ flow\b", q):
            has_flow_intent = True
        if _re.search(r"\bexplain the .+ flow\b", q):
            has_flow_intent = True

        has_code_localization_intent = any(term in q for term in code_localization_terms)

        signal_action_terms = [
            "signal",
            "slot",
            "connects",
            "connected to",
            "signal handler",
            "signal connection",
            "which handler",
            "what handler",
            "which slot",
            "what slot",
            "what signal",
            "which signal",
        ]

        has_signal_action_intent = any(term in q for term in signal_action_terms)

        widget_listing_terms = [
            "list all buttons",
            "list all widgets",
            "list the buttons",
            "list the widgets",
            "all buttons with",
            "all buttons and",
            "buttons with their",
            "buttons and their",
            "widgets with their",
            "widgets and their",
        ]
        has_widget_listing_intent = any(term in q for term in widget_listing_terms)

        return (
                has_explanation_intent
                or has_flow_intent
                or has_code_localization_intent
                or has_signal_action_intent
                or has_widget_listing_intent
        )

This function resets the EEG visualization pipeline state, stopping timers, disconnecting signals, rolling back to an initial snapshot, and hiding UI elements (evidence [S01]).

Below is a line-level snippet of its body from [SN01]:

def eeg_visu_reset_snpsht(viz_core) -> None:
 # --- ensure snapshot slot exists -----------------------------------------
 if not hasattr(viz_core, "_controls_lane_state"):
 viz_core._controls_lane_state = None

 # --- 0) Stop timers & disconnect volatile signals ------------------------
 try:
 if hasattr(viz_core, "plot_timer") and viz_core.plot_timer is not None:
 viz_core.plot_timer.stop()
 except Exception:
 logger.debug("plot_timer.stop() failed", exc_info=True)

 # timeline moved signal (disconnect all slots to prevent spurious updates)
 try:
 if hasattr(viz_core, "timeline_moved"):
 viz_core.timeline_moved.disconnect()
 except Exception:
 # harmless if there were no connections
 pass

 # --- 1) Roll back visualizer state to initial snapshot -------------------
 from core.snapshot.eeg_session_snapshot import (
 reset_to_initial_eeg_state,
 )

The next patch

Add a second-pass callee expansion after the first retrieval pass.
Goal

When a top snippet contains a direct call or import of a named function, and that function exists in symbol/file indexes, pull that callee into the final evidence bundle.
Minimal algorithm

In v10_retriever.py:

import re

CALL_RE = re.compile(r"\b([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*)\s*\(")
FROM_IMPORT_RE = re.compile(r"from\s+[\w\.]+\s+import\s+\(\s*([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*)", re.MULTILINE)
SIMPLE_IMPORT_RE = re.compile(r"from\s+[\w\.]+\s+import\s+([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*)")

IGNORE_CALLS = {
    "hasattr", "getattr", "setattr", "isinstance", "len", "print",
    "dict", "list", "set", "tuple", "int", "str", "float", "bool",
}

def _extract_named_callees(snippet_text: str) -> set[str]:
    names = set()

    for m in CALL_RE.finditer(snippet_text or ""):
        name = m.group(1)
        if name not in IGNORE_CALLS and not name.startswith("_re"):
            names.add(name)

    for m in FROM_IMPORT_RE.finditer(snippet_text or ""):
        names.add(m.group(1))

    for m in SIMPLE_IMPORT_RE.finditer(snippet_text or ""):
        names.add(m.group(1))

    return names

Then after your first retrieval pass:

top_snippets = final_bundle.snippet_evidence[:8]
candidate_callees = set()

for sn in top_snippets:
    candidate_callees |= _extract_named_callees(getattr(sn, "content", "") or getattr(sn, "text", ""))

# keep only project-relevant names
candidate_callees = {
    name for name in candidate_callees
    if name in self.index.symbol_index
}

Then boost those symbols/files into the final ranking:

    add symbol hits for each callee

    add snippet hits for snippets anchored to those symbols

    rebuild the final bundle

Ranking preference

For flow/reset questions, boost these kinds of callees:

    reset_*

    capture_*

    cleanup_*

    *_to_initial_*

    handle_*

But down-rank noise like:

    hasattr

    getattr

    widget .hide()

    logging calls

Why this is the right next step

Your latest symbol-specific query already showed the real chain:

    on_close_clicked

    eeg_visu_reset_snpsht

    reset_to_initial_eeg_state

So now the system needs to follow that chain automatically during broad trace questions.
One more improvement

Your “direct calls” answer included noisy builtins like hasattr and getattr. That is a second, smaller issue. Later, refine “direct calls” to prefer:

    project functions

    imported reset/cleanup functions

    method calls that change state

and ignore utility/builtin calls unless the user explicitly asks for all calls.

2) Insert the callee-expansion logic inside retrieve(...)

Your current retrieve(...) does:

    _retrieve_files(...)

    _retrieve_symbols(...)

    _retrieve_snippets(...)

    then returns RetrievalBundle(...) directly

So there is no final_bundle variable in the live file. Also, your retriever uses self.idx, not self.index, and the symbol map used elsewhere in the file is self.idx.symbol_details, not self.index.symbol_index.

That means you should insert the callee logic after this block:

file_evidence = self._retrieve_files(question, effective_file_limit)
symbol_evidence = self._retrieve_symbols(question, effective_symbol_limit)
snippet_evidence = self._retrieve_snippets(
    question,
    file_evidence,
    symbol_evidence,
    effective_snippet_limit,
)

and before this return:

return RetrievalBundle(
    file_evidence=file_evidence,
    symbol_evidence=symbol_evidence,
    snippet_evidence=snippet_evidence,

So the insertion point is exactly:

file_evidence = self._retrieve_files(question, effective_file_limit)

    question,

    symbol_evidence,
    effective_snippet_limit,
)

    file_evidence=file_evidence,
    symbol_evidence=symbol_evidence,

if candidate_callees:
    expanded_question = question + " " + " ".join(sorted(candidate_callees))

        max_symbols=max(effective_symbol_limit, symbol_limit + 4),
        max_snippets=max(effective_snippet_limit, snippet_limit + 4),
    )

    file_evidence = merged.file_evidence
    symbol_evidence = merged.symbol_evidence
    snippet_evidence = merged.snippet_evidence

That gives you a minimal second pass without rewriting the retriever architecture.
5) Final shape of retrieve(...)

So your retrieve(...) should end up like this:

file_evidence = self._retrieve_files(question, effective_file_limit)
symbol_evidence = self._retrieve_symbols(question, effective_symbol_limit)
snippet_evidence = self._retrieve_snippets(
    question,
    file_evidence,
    symbol_evidence,
    effective_snippet_limit,
)

top_snippets = snippet_evidence[:8]
candidate_callees: set[str] = set()

for sn in top_snippets:
    candidate_callees |= _extract_named_callees(
        str(sn.get("text", "") or sn.get("content", ""))
    )

candidate_callees = {
    name for name in candidate_callees
    if any(
        name == sym.split(".")[-1]
        or name == sym
        for sym in self.idx.symbol_details.keys()
    )
}

if candidate_callees:
    expanded_question = question + " " + " ".join(sorted(candidate_callees))

    expanded_symbol_evidence = self._retrieve_symbols(
        expanded_question,
        max(effective_symbol_limit, symbol_limit + 4),
    )
    expanded_snippet_evidence = self._retrieve_snippets(
        expanded_question,
        file_evidence,
        expanded_symbol_evidence,
        max(effective_snippet_limit, snippet_limit + 4),
    )

    merged = self.merge_with_previous(
        RetrievalBundle(
            file_evidence=file_evidence,
            symbol_evidence=expanded_symbol_evidence,
            snippet_evidence=expanded_snippet_evidence,
        ),
        RetrievalBundle(
            file_evidence=file_evidence,
            symbol_evidence=symbol_evidence,
            snippet_evidence=snippet_evidence,
        ),
        max_files=max(effective_file_limit, file_limit),
        max_symbols=max(effective_symbol_limit, symbol_limit + 4),
        max_snippets=max(effective_snippet_limit, snippet_limit + 4),
    )

    file_evidence = merged.file_evidence
    symbol_evidence = merged.symbol_evidence
    snippet_evidence = merged.snippet_evidence

return RetrievalBundle(
    file_evidence=file_evidence,
    symbol_evidence=symbol_evidence,
    snippet_evidence=snippet_evidence,
)

Replace this function in all three files:

    v10_retriever.py

    v10_query_router.py

    v10_prompt_builder.py

with this:

def is_runtime_heavy_question(q: str) -> bool:
    q = norm_text(q)

    generic_terms = [
        "runtime",
        "signal",
        "slot",
        "connect",
        "disconnect",
        "emit",
        "timer",
        "thread",
        "event loop",
        "callback",
        "handler",
        "listener",
        "probe",
        "trace at runtime",
        "runtime trace",
        "execution at runtime",
    ]

    if any(term in q for term in generic_terms):
        return True

    # Generic callback-style names such as on_click, on_submit, on_close, etc.
    if re.search(r"\bon_[a-z0-9_]+\b", q):
        return True

    return False

Then remove the repo-specific callback/probe literals from runtime intent:

    snapshot

    clicked

    textchanged

    text changed

Then remove the repo-specific callback/probe literals from runtime intent:

snapshot
clicked

Yes — for runtime intent only, those literals should now be gone from:

    v10_retriever.py

    v10_query_router.py

    v10_prompt_builder.py

What “remove them” means exactly

You do not need to hunt them everywhere in the repo.

You only remove them from the old is_runtime_heavy_question(...) detector logic, where they were acting as generic runtime triggers.

So these should no longer appear inside that function’s term list:

snapshot
clicked
textchanged
text changed
on_apply_clicked
on_text_changed
on_close_clicked
runtime_runner_probe_apply_button
runtime_runner_probe_line_edit
runtime_runner_probe_close_button

Because those are:

    either project-specific

    or too broad/noisy for a generic runtime classifier

Why each one should be removed

snapshot
Not inherently runtime. In many repos it is just persistence/state terminology.

clicked
Too broad. A UI question about a button label could accidentally look “runtime-heavy”.

textchanged / text changed
Also too broad and UI-specific.

on_apply_clicked / on_text_changed / on_close_clicked
These are repo-specific callback names. The generic regex on_[a-z0-9_]+ replaces them cleanly.

runtime_runner_probe_apply_button / runtime_runner_probe_line_edit / runtime_runner_probe_close_button
These are highly project-specific probe artifacts and should never live in core intent logic.
What should replace them

The new function handles runtime intent in a generic way:

def is_runtime_heavy_question(q: str) -> bool:
    q = norm_text(q)

    generic_terms = [
        "runtime",
        "signal",
        "slot",
        "connect",
        "disconnect",
        "emit",
        "timer",
        "thread",
        "event loop",
        "callback",
        "handler",
        "listener",
        "probe",
        "trace at runtime",
        "runtime trace",
        "execution at runtime",
    ]

    if any(term in q for term in generic_terms):
        return True

    if re.search(r"\bon_[a-z0-9_]+\b", q):
        return True

    return False

Important boundary

Do not remove those literals from:

    project profile aliases

    runtime anchor builders

    repo-specific debug helpers

    real symbol matching code

Remove them only from the generic runtime-intent detector.

 """
 Restore the visualizer to its startup EEG state.
 This clears active EEG data, plot artists, timeline state, amplitude-map
 elements, and tab-local controls that should disappear once an EEG is closed.
 """
 if not hasattr(visualizer, "_eeg_snapshot"):
 logger.warning("No EEG snapshot available to restore")
 return

 logger.info("Resetting EEG to initial snapshot")

 _cleanup_amplitude_map(visualizer)
 _reset_amplitude_map_button(visualizer)
 cleanup_all_artists(visualizer)

The flow from the close action to the final cleanup methods is as follows:
-
EEGVisualizerCore.on_close_clicked (close action entry point) calls reset_to_initial_eeg_state.
- reset_to_initial_eeg_state performs cleanup actions like _cleanup_amplitude_map, _reset_amplitude_map_button, cleanup_all_artists, and _remove_ruler_axis.
- reset_to_initial_eeg_state also handles axis decorations cleanup via _clear_axis_y_decorations and log_tick_labels.
- Finally, it restores the snapshot data to reset the visualizer state.

The evidence shows the entire flow, but the exact body of some cleanup utility functions (e.g., _cleanup_amplitude_map) is not present in the SOURCE SNIPPETS.

Trace the runtime signal/slot flow when the EEG close action is triggered. Show the handler, connected signals, and runtime callbacks involved.

When the EEG close action is triggered, the runtime signal/slot flow begins with EEGVisualizerCore.on_close_clicked. According to [F01], the close button signal tb.close_eeg_requested is connected to this handler method via _wire_close_button. The handler itself, located at shell/viewer/eeg_visualizer_core.py:193 ([S01]), simply calls eeg_visu_reset_snpsht(self). The reset function, defined at core/snapshot/eeg_pipeline_reset.py:6 ([S08]), executes a sequence of cleanup steps: it stops plot timers, disconnects the timeline_moved signal, and rolls back the visualizer state to its initial snapshot via reset_to_initial_eeg_state. These steps ensure memory is reclaimed and the UI is restored. No further callbacks or signal emissions are shown in the retrieved evidence.

What happens at runtime when on_close_clicked executes? Show the callback flow, connected signals, and immediate downstream calls.

When on_close_clicked is called on an EEGVisualizerCore instance, it directly invokes eeg_visu_reset_snpsht, which is defined in core/snapshot/eeg_pipeline_reset.py (see [F09] and [S09]). The eeg_visu_reset_snpsht function handles restoring the startup snapshot, stopping any active timers, disconnecting volatile signals, and clearing EEG data and UI elements. It does not trigger any Qt signals; instead, it directly manipulates the core’s state. According to [F14] in shell/viewer/eeg_display_launcher.py, the tb.close_eeg_requested signal is connected to core.on_close_clicked, so the callback flow is initiated by that signal. The immediate downstream call from on_close_clicked is eeg_visu_reset_snpsht (see [SN01]).

Explain reset_to_initial_eeg_state with code. Show the function body as line-level snippets, then list only the direct calls inside that function.

python
def reset_to_initial_eeg_state(visualizer):
 """
 Restore the visualizer to its startup EEG state.

 This clears active EEG data, plot artists, timeline state, amplitude-map
 elements, and tab-local controls that should disappear once an EEG is
 closed.
 """
 if not hasattr(visualizer, "_eeg_snapshot"):
 logger.warning("No EEG snapshot available to restore")
 return

 logger.info("Resetting EEG to initial snapshot")

 _cleanup_amplitude_map(visualizer)
 _reset_amplitude_map_button(visualizer)
 cleanup_all_artists(visualizer)
 _remove_ruler_axis(visualizer)

 if hasattr(visualizer, "ax"):
 _clear_axis_y_decorations(getattr(visualizer, "ax", None), "main axis")

 if hasattr(visualizer, "label_ax"):
 _clear_axis_y_decorations(
 getattr(visualizer, "label_ax", None),
 "label axis",
 )

Which module is responsible for restoring EEG state to its initial snapshot, and what methods participate in that responsibility split?

The module responsible for restoring EEG state to its initial snapshot is core.snapshot.eeg_session_snapshot (defined in the file [F10] and evidenced by snippet [SN06] at line 202 in core/snapshot/eeg_session_snapshot.py).

The main method performing the restoration is reset_to_initial_eeg_state, which is declared in [SN06] and documented as restoring the visualizer to its startup EEG state by clearing active EEG data, plot artists, timeline state, and amplitude-map elements. It directly invokes helper methods such as _cleanup_amplitude_map, _reset_amplitude_map_button, cleanup_all_artists, and _remove_ruler_axis, but the evidence pack does not contain the source snippets for those helper methods.

Additionally, the responsibility is split with shell/viewer/eeg_visualizer_core.py (file [F04] and method [S10] at line 193), where the on_close_clicked method calls eeg_visu_reset_snpsht(self). The evidence pack does not provide a snippet for eeg_visu_reset_snpsht, so its exact mapping to reset_to_initial_eeg_state is not explicitly documented, although the docstring states it restores the startup snapshot.

    def _grounding_failure_reason(self, prompt: str, answer_text: str) -> str:
        if self._is_deterministic_prompt(prompt):
            if self._looks_ungrounded_deterministic(answer_text):
                return "looks_ungrounded_deterministic"
            if self._has_forbidden_ids(prompt, answer_text):
                return "forbidden_ids"
            if self._has_forbidden_paths(prompt, answer_text):
                return "forbidden_paths"
            return ""

        if self._is_generative_prompt(prompt):
            if self._answer_ignored_required_snippets(prompt, answer_text):
                return "ignored_required_snippets"
            if self._looks_ungrounded_generative(answer_text):
                return "looks_ungrounded_generative"
            if self._has_forbidden_ids(prompt, answer_text):
                return "forbidden_ids"
            if self._has_forbidden_paths(prompt, answer_text):
                return "forbidden_paths"

            enforce_forbidden_symbols = not self._is_code_localized_prompt(prompt)
            if enforce_forbidden_symbols and self._has_forbidden_symbols(prompt, answer_text):
                return "forbidden_symbols"

            return ""

            return "looks_ungrounded_default"
        if self._has_forbidden_ids(prompt, answer_text):
            return "forbidden_ids"
        if self._has_forbidden_paths(prompt, answer_text):
            return "forbidden_paths"
        if self._has_forbidden_symbols(prompt, answer_text):
            return "forbidden_symbols"
        return ""

Step 1 — change this one line in _grounding_failure_reason(...)

)

So that part becomes:

if self._is_generative_prompt(prompt):
    if self._answer_ignored_required_snippets(prompt, answer_text):
        return "ignored_required_snippets"
    if self._looks_ungrounded_generative(answer_text):
        return "looks_ungrounded_generative"
    if self._has_forbidden_ids(prompt, answer_text):
        return "forbidden_ids"
    if self._has_forbidden_paths(prompt, answer_text):
        return "forbidden_paths"

        self._is_code_localized_prompt(prompt)
        or self._is_runtime_heavy_prompt(prompt)

Step 2 — add the new helper method

Add this new method near the other prompt classifiers, ideally:

    below _is_code_localized_prompt(...)

    or above _is_generative_prompt(...)

Use this:

def _is_runtime_heavy_prompt(self, prompt: str) -> bool:
    q = self._extract_user_question(prompt)

    runtime_terms = [
        "runtime",
        "signal",
        "slot",
        "connect",
        "disconnect",
        "emit",
        "timer",
        "thread",
        "event loop",
        "callback",
        "handler",
        "listener",
        "probe",
        "trace at runtime",
        "runtime trace",
        "execution at runtime",
    ]

    if any(term in q for term in runtime_terms):
        return True

    if re.search(r"\bon_[a-z0-9_]+\b", q):
        return True

    return False

Important check

To show the runtime flow for signal connections, timer stop, and disconnect behavior during EEG reset, we rely on [F01], [F02], [F03], [F04], [F05], and [SN02].

During EEG reset, the runtime flow begins with EEGVisualizerCore.on_close_clicked (defined in shell/viewer/eeg_visualizer_core.py at line 193, as shown in [SN01]), which directly calls eeg_visu_reset_snpsht(viz_core).     def _retrieve_snippets(
            self,
            question: str,
            file_evidence: list[EvidenceItem],
            symbol_evidence: list[SymbolEvidenceItem],
            limit: int,
    ) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
        q = norm_text(question)

        snippets: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
        seen: set[tuple[str, int, str]] = set()

        # Primary path: serve snippets from pre-indexed JSON, not disk.
        snippets_by_file = getattr(self.idx, "snippets_by_file", {})
        if snippets_by_file:
            for item in symbol_evidence:
                if not is_allowed_project_path(item.path):
                    continue

                candidates = snippets_by_file.get(item.path, [])
                if not candidates:
                    continue

                symbol_full = str(item.symbol_name or "").strip()
                symbol_tail = symbol_full.split(".")[-1].strip().lower()

                # Prefer exact symbol match first.
                preferred = [
                    rec for rec in candidates
                    if str(rec.get("symbol_name", "")).strip().lower() == symbol_full.lower()
                       or str(rec.get("symbol_name", "")).split(".")[-1].strip().lower() == symbol_tail
                ]

                selected_candidates = preferred if preferred else candidates

                for rec in selected_candidates:
                    line = int(rec.get("line", 1) or 1)
                    key = (item.path, line, symbol_full)
                    if key in seen:
                        continue

                    text = str(rec.get("snippet", "") or "").rstrip()
                    if not text:
                        continue

                    seen.add(key)
                    snippets.append({
                        "snippet_id": "SN" + str(len(snippets) + 1).zfill(2),
                        "path": item.path,
                        "line": line,
                        "anchor": symbol_full or str(rec.get("symbol_name", "")),
                        "text": text,
                    })

                    if len(snippets) >= limit:
                        return snippets

Paste the compile result and that answer back, and I’ll give you the next single step.

During EEG reset, the runtime flow for signal connections, timer stop, and disconnect behavior involves several key methods and signals. The process begins when the EEG close action is triggered, leading to the invocation of EEGVisualizerCore.on_close_clicked (shell/viewer/eeg_visualizer_core.py:193, [S01], [SN01]). This method calls eeg_visu_reset_snpsht (core/snapshot/eeg_pipeline_reset.py:6, [S02], [SN02]) to handle the reset logic.

eeg_visu_reset_snpsht performs several critical actions:
1. It stops the plot_timer if it exists and is not None (lines 27-30 in [SN02]).
2. It disconnects the timeline_moved signal to prevent spurious updates (lines 33-38 in [SN02]).
3. It then calls reset_to_initial_eeg_state to restore the visualizer state to its initial snapshot (lines 41-43 in [SN02]).

Additionally, other components are involved in managing signal connections and disconnections:
- SignalManager.init_signals (shell/viewer/eeg_signal_manager.py:20, [S09]) connects Qt signals to their handlers, but this is part of initialization rather than reset.
- EEGAmplitudeMap.disconnect_signals (plugins/amplitude_map/eeg_amplitude_map.py:29, [F09]) disconnects signals related to EEG data loading, though this is not directly called during reset in the provided evidence.
- ComboCaptionAdapter.detach (plugins/filter_panel/combo_caption_adapter.py:107, [F05]) disconnects various signals related to combo boxes, but its role in reset is not directly linked in the evidence.

The evidence shows that timer stopping and signal disconnection are handled within eeg_visu_reset_snpsht, but the specifics of signal reconnection during reset are not fully detailed in the provided snippets.

Paste that next answer back.

During EEG reset, the runtime flow for signal connections, timer stop, and disconnect behavior is governed by the following evidence-backed steps.

The reset process is triggered by EEGVisualizerCore.on_close_clicked, defined in shell/viewer/eeg_visualizer_core.py:193 ([S01]). This method invokes the central reset function eeg_visu_reset_snpsht ([S02], [SN02]).

Inside eeg_visu_reset_snpsht ([F10], [S02], [SN02]), the flow begins with stopping timers and disconnecting volatile signals:
- If viz_core.plot_timer exists, it is stopped (viz_core.plot_timer.stop()).
- The timeline_moved signal is disconnected if present (viz_core.timeline_moved.disconnect()).

Next, the reset function delegates to reset_to_initial_eeg_state (imported from core.snapshot.eeg_session_snapshot but not included in snippets). This step is responsible for rolling back visualizer state to the initial snapshot. Within that scope, _cleanup_amplitude_map is called ([F07]), which disconnects the eeg_loaded_signal from its previous target (visualizer.eeg_loaded_signal.disconnect -> old_target.on_eeg_loaded).

The amplitude map initialization logic, in shell/viewer/eeg_amplitude_map_initializer.py, ensures signal connections are managed precisely once during reset ([F03], [S03], [SN03]). The function _ensure_amplitude_map_signal_connected explicitly disconnects any previous on_eeg_loaded handler before connecting the new one (core.eeg_loaded_signal.disconnect(old_target.on_eeg_loaded) followed by core.eeg_loaded_signal.connect(amp_map.on_eeg_loaded)). This mechanism prevents stale signal handlers from persisting across resets.

Additionally, EEGAmplitudeMap.disconnect_signals ([F09]) disconnects the amplitude map’s own signal handlers (self.vis.eeg_loaded_signal.disconnect -> self.on_eeg_loaded) and manages drag handler connections (self.drag.disconnect -> self.vis.canvas).

Missing link: The evidence does not show the exact calls inside reset_to_initial_eeg_state or how viz_core.timeline_moved is initially connected.

The runtime flow for signal connections, timer stop, and disconnect behavior during EEG reset proceeds as follows:

1. When the close button in the main window toolbar is clicked, the Qt signal tb.close_eeg_requested triggers the slot core.on_close_clicked (as wired in eeg_display_launcher.py [F01] and cited in [SN01]).

2. EEGVisualizerCore.on_close_clicked (shell/viewer/eeg_visualizer_core.py:193, [S01], [SN01]) delegates to eeg_visu_reset_snpsht (core/snapshot/eeg_pipeline_reset.py:6, [S02], [SN02]).

3. eeg_visu_reset_snpsht performs several cleanup steps in the specified order:
 a. It stops the plot timer if present, with no evidence of further timer-related actions.
 b. It disconnects the viz_core.timeline_moved signal via viz_core.timeline_moved.disconnect(), intended to remove all slots and prevent spurious updates ([SN02] lines 33–38).

4. After disconnecting the timeline signal, eeg_visu_reset_snpsht then invokes reset_to_initial_eeg_state (core/snapshot/eeg_session_snapshot.py:202, [S03], [SN03]), which cleans up amplitude-map state and artists but does not directly disconnect any further signals ([SN03] lines 216–234).

No snapshot restores signal connections or starts timers again during reset; the UI scaffolding (main window, canvas widget) remains intact so reopen works ([SN02] docstring bullet 18).

The runtime flow for signal connections, timer stop, and disconnect behavior during EEG reset begins when the user triggers the EEG close action, which calls EEGVisualizerCore.on_close_clicked in shell/viewer/eeg_visualizer_core.py (line 193) as shown in [SN01]. Paste the next answer back.

During EEG reset, the runtime flow for signal connections, timer stop, and disconnect behavior is as follows:

1. When the EEG close action is triggered, the method EEGVisualizerCore.on_close_clicked (defined in shell/viewer/eeg_visualizer_core.py:193, [SN01]) is called. This method invokes the function eeg_visu_reset_snpsht (defined in core/snapshot/eeg_pipeline_reset.py:6, [SN02]) to reset the visualizer to its initial state.

2. The function eeg_visu_reset_snpsht first ensures any previous controls lane state exists, then proceeds to stop timers and disconnect volatile signals. Specifically, it stops the plot_timer if it exists (lines 27-30, [SN02]), and disconnects the timeline_moved signal (lines 33-38, [SN02]) to prevent spurious updates.

3. After stopping timers and disconnecting signals, eeg_visu_reset_snpsht calls reset_to_initial_eeg_state (defined in core/snapshot/eeg_session_snapshot.py:202, [SN03]) to restore the visualizer to its startup EEG state. This function clears active EEG data, plot artists, timeline state, amplitude-map elements, and tab-local controls. It disables amplitude map buttons, hides tab toolbars and buttons, and resets axes decorations.

4. The reset_to_initial_eeg_state function also invokes internal helpers like _cleanup_amplitude_map and cleanup_all_artists, but these do not involve further signal disconnects or timer stops beyond what eeg_visu_reset_snpsht already handles.

Thus, the flow is: on_close_clicked → eeg_visu_reset_snpsht (stops timers and disconnects signals) → reset_to_initial_eeg_state (restores UI state). Missing is explicit evidence of additional disconnections beyond timeline_moved and plot_timer.

    def _retrieve_files(self, question: str, limit: int) -> list[EvidenceItem]:
        q = norm_text(question)
        tokens = tokenize_query(q)
        intents = detect_query_intents(q)

        explanation_heavy = (
                intents["explanatory"] or intents["code_localized_explanation"] or intents["explain_chain"]
        )
        is_runtime_question = intents["runtime_heavy"]

        entry_files = {
            path for path in self.idx.project_summary.get("entry_files", [])
            if is_allowed_project_path(path)
        }

        question_file_names = set(re.findall(r"[a-zA-Z0-9_\-]+\.py", q))
        scored: list[EvidenceItem] = []

        exact_reference_query = is_where_is_called_question(q)
        exact_symbol_targets = [
            token for token in tokens
            if "_" in token or "." in token or len(token) >= 10
        ]

        wants_call_site = (
                "call site" in q
                or "called" in q
                or "constructs" in q
                or "startup chain" in q
                or self._question_has_profile_alias(q, "top_navigation")
        )

        wants_definition = (
                "files define" in q
                or "which files define" in q
                or "defined by" in q
                or "where is defined" in q
                or "definition" in q
        )

        asks_for_navbar_builder = self._question_has_profile_alias(
            q,
            "navbar_builder_terms",
        )

        asks_for_notebook_builder = self._question_has_profile_alias(
            q,
            "notebook_builder_terms",
        )

        reset_cleanup_query = (
                "reset" in q
                or "cleanup" in q
                or "snapshot" in q
                or "close" in q
                or self._question_has_profile_alias(q, "reset_cleanup")
        )

        candidate_paths = set(self.idx.files_by_path.keys())

        if is_runtime_question:
            candidate_paths.update(self.idx.runtime_events_by_file.keys())

        # Do not add their source files to candidate_paths - they are not
        # EEG project files and would inject empty-content noise into evidence.

        for path in sorted(candidate_paths):
            if not is_allowed_project_path(path):
                continue

            file_record = self.idx.files_by_path.get(path, {})
            if not isinstance(file_record, dict):
                file_record = {}

            module_name = file_record.get(
                "module_name",
                path[:-3].replace("\\", ".").replace("/", ".") if path.endswith(".py") else path,
            )
            docstring = file_record.get("docstring", "")
            strings = " ".join(file_record.get("strings", []))
            comments = " ".join(file_record.get("comments", []))
            entry_markers = " ".join(file_record.get("entry_markers", []))
            ui_hits = " ".join(file_record.get("ui_keyword_hits", []))
            eeg_hits = " ".join(file_record.get("eeg_keyword_hits", []))
            semantic_hints = " ".join(file_record.get("semantic_hints", []))
            roles = self.idx.semantic_roles.get(path, {}).get("roles", [])

            function_names: list[str] = []
            class_names: list[str] = []
            called_symbols: list[str] = []

            for fn in file_record.get("functions", []):
                function_names.append(fn.get("qualname", ""))
                function_names.append(fn.get("name", ""))
                for call in fn.get("calls", []):
                    called_symbols.append(call.get("call_name", ""))

The runtime flow for signal connections, timer stop, and disconnect behavior during EEG reset proceeds as follows.

When the user clicks the close button, the method EEGVisualizerCore.on_close_clicked in shell/viewer/eeg_visualizer_core.py:193 is invoked. This method calls eeg_visu_reset_snpsht from core/snapshot/eeg_pipeline_reset.py:6.

Inside eeg_visu_rest_snpsht, the reset logic first stops any active plot timer via plot_timer.stop() and disconnects the timeline moved signal via timeline_moved.disconnect(). These actions are performed in a try-except block to safely handle any connection errors. According to [F08] and [SN02], these disconnection steps target volatile signals so that spurious updates do not occur after the EEG session closes.

Following the timer stop and disconnect phase, eeg_visu_reset_snpsht then calls reset_to_initial_eeg_state from core/snapshot/eeg_session_snapshot.py:202. This function is responsible for restoring the visualizer to its startup state, including clearing axes decorations, removing artists, and rolling back amplitude map controls.

During the reset flow, no new signal connections are established after the disconnection and cleanup steps. The evidence shows only disconnections and cleanup, not reconnections. Thus, the runtime flow involves disconnecting signals, stopping timers, cleaning up renderers, and restoring the initial snapshot state without re-connecting signals until a new session is explicitly initialized.

The runtime flow for signal connections, timer stop, and disconnect behavior during EEG reset is as follows:

1. When the user triggers the EEG close action (for example, by clicking a close button), the method EEGVisualizerCore.on_close_clicked is invoked ([SN01] from shell/viewer/eeg_visualizer_core.py:193).

2. on_close_clicked calls eeg_visu_reset_snpsht(self) ([SN01], [S01]).

3. eeg_visu_reset_snpsht is a function defined in core/snapshot/eeg_pipeline_reset.py:6 ([SN02], [S02]). This function is responsible for resetting the EEG visualizer state by stopping timers and disconnecting volatile signals. Specifically:
 - It stops the plot_timer if it exists ([SN02] lines 26–30).
 - It disconnects the timeline_moved signal from all its slots to prevent spurious updates ([SN02] lines 32–38).

4. After stopping timers and disconnecting signals, eeg_visu_reset_snpsht calls reset_to_initial_eeg_state(self) to restore the visualizer to its initial state ([SN02], [S02], [S03]). This function clears active EEG data, plot artists, timeline state, amplitude-map elements, and tab-local controls ([SN03] from core/snapshot/eeg_session_snapshot.py:202). The actual signal disconnections are not explicitly shown in the snippet, but the code implies cleanup of signal-related resources.

5. The flow does not explicitly show further disconnections beyond the timer and timeline signals mentioned. The evidence is partial regarding whether other signals (e.g., eeg_loaded_signal) are disconnected during this reset phase.

Additionally, it checks for a timeline_moved signal and disconnects all slots connected to it using viz_core.timeline_moved.disconnect(). ([SN02], lines 27-38)

There is no explicit evidence of other signals being disconnected during this reset flow. ([SN02], lines 27 and 34).

The runtime flow for signal connections, timer stop, and disconnect behavior during EEG reset begins when the user triggers the close action, which invokes EEGVisualizerCore.on_close_clicked (shell/viewer/eeg_visualizer_core.py:193 [SN01] [S01]).

This method calls eeg_visu_reset_snpsht (core/snapshot/eeg_pipeline_reset.py:6 [SN02] [S02]), which performs the reset steps in two phases:

1. **Timer stop and signal disconnection** (lines 26–38 in [SN02]):
 - Checks for the presence of the plot_timer and stops it using viz_core.plot_timer.stop() to halt periodic plotting updates.
 - Disconnects the timeline_moved signal using viz_core.timeline_moved.disconnect() to prevent spurious updates and reduce memory churn.

2. **State restoration via snapshot** (line 41 ff. in [SN02]):
 - Invokes reset_to_initial_eeg_state (core/snapshot/eeg_session_snapshot.py:202 [SN03] [S03]) with the visualizer instance.
 - This function cleans up amplitude map elements, resets axes decorations, and restores the visualizer attributes from an earlier snapshot (visualizer.raw and visualizer.data are set from the snapshot), effectively reverting the UI to a state without loaded EEG data.

No additional disconnections or timer-related steps appear in the provided evidence for the reset flow.

        reason="Exact call-site lookup question.",
    )

    ...

That order is the problem.

Because your question contains:

    Which method calls ...

    and also

    what exact snippet shows that call site

the router should not stop at the deterministic which_calls rule.
Step 2 — exact replacement

Replace this block:

if is_which_calls_question(q):
    return QueryRouteDecision(
        route="deterministic",
        intent_name="which_calls",
        reason="Exact call-site lookup question.",
    )

with this:

if is_which_calls_question(q):
    if (
        is_code_localization_question(q)
        or is_explanatory_question(q)
        or "snippet" in q
        or "snippets" in q
        or "call site" in q
        or "code" in q
        or "what exact snippet shows" in q
    ):
        return QueryRouteDecision(
            route="generative",
            intent_name="which_calls_with_code",
            reason="Caller lookup question also requests snippet/code evidence.",
        )

    return QueryRouteDecision(
        route="deterministic",
        intent_name="which_calls",
        reason="Exact call-site lookup question.",
    )

So the top becomes:

def _retrieve_snippets(
        self,
        question: str,
        file_evidence: list[EvidenceItem],
        symbol_evidence: list[SymbolEvidenceItem],
        limit: int,
) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
    q = norm_text(question)

    reset_runtime_query = (
        is_runtime_heavy_question(q)
        and (
            "reset" in q
            or "close" in q
            or "disconnect" in q
            or "timer" in q
            or "signal" in q
        )
    )

    which_calls_query = is_which_calls_question(q)

    exact_call_targets = []
    if which_calls_query:
        for token in tokenize_query(q):
            if ("_" in token or "." in token) and len(token) >= 6:
                for sym in self.idx.symbol_details.keys():
                    sym_tail = sym.split(".")[-1].lower()
                    if token == sym.lower() or token == sym_tail:
                        exact_call_targets.append(token)
                        break

    snippets: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
    seen: set[tuple[str, int, str]] = set()

Step 2 — front-load real call-site snippets before the normal symbol loop

You currently have this primary pre-indexed block:

    # Primary path: serve snippets from pre-indexed JSON, not disk.
    snippets_by_file = getattr(self.idx, "snippets_by_file", {})
    if snippets_by_file:
        for item in symbol_evidence:
            ...

Insert the new call-site scan inside if snippets_by_file: and before for item in symbol_evidence:.

Add this:

            if which_calls_query and exact_call_targets:
                preferred_paths = {item.path for item in file_evidence if is_allowed_project_path(item.path)}

                for path in sorted(preferred_paths or snippets_by_file.keys()):
                    if not is_allowed_project_path(path):
                        continue

                    candidates = snippets_by_file.get(path, [])
                    if not candidates:
                        continue

                    for rec in candidates:
                        line = int(rec.get("line", 1) or 1)
                        symbol_name = str(rec.get("symbol_name", "") or "").strip()
                        symbol_tail = symbol_name.split(".")[-1].strip().lower()
                        text = str(rec.get("snippet", "") or "").rstrip()
                        if not text:
                            continue

                        text_norm = text.lower()

                        matched_target = None
                        for target in exact_call_targets:
                            target_tail = target.split(".")[-1].lower()

                            has_call_site = f"{target_tail}(" in text_norm
                            is_definition = f"def {target_tail}(" in text_norm

                            if has_call_site and not is_definition and symbol_tail != target_tail:
                                matched_target = target_tail
                                break

                        if not matched_target:
                            continue

                        key = (path, line, symbol_name or matched_target)
                        if key in seen:
                            continue

                        seen.add(key)
                        snippets.append({
                            "snippet_id": "SN" + str(len(snippets) + 1).zfill(2),
                            "path": path,
                            "line": line,
                            "anchor": symbol_name or matched_target,
                            "text": text,
                        })

                        if len(snippets) >= limit:
                            return snippets

So the structure becomes:

    # Primary path: serve snippets from pre-indexed JSON, not disk.
    snippets_by_file = getattr(self.idx, "snippets_by_file", {})
    if snippets_by_file:
        if which_calls_query and exact_call_targets:
            ...
            # new call-site scan here

        for item in symbol_evidence:
            ...

This patch changes the order of evidence gathering for that question type:

    first find snippets that literally contain reset_to_initial_eeg_state(

    exclude def reset_to_initial_eeg_state( so we do not mistake the definition for the call site

    only then continue into the usual symbol-based snippet retrieval

That is exactly what this question needs.

Yes — which_calls_query must be defined inside retrieve(...), not just elsewhere.

Use this updated version of the top of retrieve(...):

def retrieve(
    self,
    question: str,
    *,
    file_limit: int = 10,
    symbol_limit: int = 10,
    snippet_limit: int = 6,
) -> RetrievalBundle:

    q = norm_text(question)
    intents = detect_query_intents(q)
    which_calls_query = intents["which_calls"]

    effective_file_limit = file_limit
    effective_symbol_limit = symbol_limit
    effective_snippet_limit = snippet_limit

    if intents["topomap_explanation"]:
        effective_file_limit = max(file_limit, 12)
        effective_symbol_limit = max(symbol_limit, 12)
        effective_snippet_limit = max(snippet_limit, 10)

    if intents["explanatory"] or intents["code_localized_explanation"] or intents["explain_chain"]:
        effective_file_limit = max(effective_file_limit, 12)
        effective_symbol_limit = max(effective_symbol_limit, 12)
        effective_snippet_limit = max(effective_snippet_limit, 8)

    if intents["runtime_heavy"] and (
        intents["explanatory"] or intents["code_localized_explanation"] or intents["explain_chain"]
    ):
        effective_file_limit = max(effective_file_limit, 14)
        effective_symbol_limit = max(effective_symbol_limit, 14)
        effective_snippet_limit = max(effective_snippet_limit, 10)

    file_evidence = self._retrieve_files(question, effective_file_limit)
    symbol_evidence = self._retrieve_symbols(question, effective_symbol_limit)
    snippet_evidence = self._retrieve_snippets(
        question,
        file_evidence,
        symbol_evidence,
        effective_snippet_limit,
    )

    top_snippets = snippet_evidence[:8]
    candidate_callees: set[str] = set()

    for sn in top_snippets:
        candidate_callees |= _extract_named_callees(
            str(sn.get("text", "") or sn.get("content", ""))
        )

    candidate_callees = {
        name for name in candidate_callees
        if any(
            name == sym.split(".")[-1]
            or name == sym
            for sym in self.idx.symbol_details.keys()
        )
    }

    if candidate_callees and not which_calls_query:
        expanded_question = question + " " + " ".join(sorted(candidate_callees))

Step 1 — exact place to patch

Open v10_prompt_builder.py and find:

def _build_answer_style_instructions(question: str, prefer_code: bool) -> str:

Near the top of that method, before the broad code-localized / explain / chain branches, add a new special-case branch for which calls questions.

You already have helper families in this file such as:

    _is_code_localized_explanation_question(...)

    _is_explain_implementation_question(...)

    _is_chain_or_flow_question(...)

Step 2 — insert this new branch

Put this above:

if _is_code_localized_explanation_question(question):

Insert:

    if is_which_method_calls_question(question):
        base = (
            "ANSWER STYLE\n"
            "For this question, answer as a caller-to-callee proof.\n"
            "Lead with the caller method or function, not the callee definition.\n"
            "Name the exact caller file path and caller symbol first.\n"
            "Then show the exact call-site snippet that contains the callee invocation.\n"
            "Do not answer with the callee's definition as if it were the caller.\n"
            "Do not treat a callee definition snippet as proof of who calls it.\n"
            "If multiple caller snippets are present, prefer the most direct and self-contained call site.\n"
            "If more than one caller exists, say that multiple callers are evidenced and list them in evidence order.\n"
            "If the call site is shown but the surrounding method body is partial, say the call site is shown and the full caller body is partial.\n"
            + calibration
        )

        if prefer_code:
            return (
                base
                + "You must include one short verbatim code excerpt showing the actual call site.\n"
                + "The code excerpt must contain the callee invocation itself.\n"
                + "For the excerpt, immediately state the caller file path, caller symbol, and why it proves the caller relationship.\n"
                + "Do not use the callee definition snippet unless the user separately asks where the callee is defined.\n"
                + "Do not use an import statement as the call-site proof when a direct invocation snippet is available.\n"
            )

        return (
            base
            + "Do not output code blocks unless the user explicitly asks for code.\n"
            + "Still identify the exact caller and describe the exact call-site proof in prose.\n"
        )

    caller snippets available

This new branch makes caller questions a first-class answer shape.

    the branch condition uses the wrong variable (question instead of normalized q)

    ...

Step 3 — safest full branch to use

Use this exact branch, placed immediately above if _is_code_localized_explanation_question(question):

    if is_which_method_calls_question(q):
        print("[PROMPT STYLE BRANCH]", "which_calls_with_code")

        base = (
            "ANSWER STYLE\n"
            "For this question, answer as a caller-to-callee proof.\n"
            "Lead with the caller method or function, not the callee definition.\n"
            "Name the exact caller file path and caller symbol first.\n"
            "Then show the exact call-site snippet that contains the callee invocation.\n"
            "Do not answer with the callee's definition as if it were the caller.\n"
            "Do not treat a callee definition snippet as proof of who calls it.\n"
            "If multiple caller snippets are present, prefer the most direct and self-contained call site.\n"
            "If more than one caller exists, say that multiple callers are evidenced and list them in evidence order.\n"
            "If the call site is shown but the surrounding method body is partial, say the call site is shown and the full caller body is partial.\n"
            + calibration
        )

        if prefer_code:
            return (
                base
                + "You must include one short verbatim code excerpt showing the actual call site.\n"
                + "The code excerpt must contain the callee invocation itself.\n"
                + "For the excerpt, immediately state the caller file path, caller symbol, and why it proves the caller relationship.\n"
                + "Do not use the callee definition snippet unless the user separately asks where the callee is defined.\n"
                + "Do not use an import statement as the call-site proof when a direct invocation snippet is available.\n"
            )

        return (
            base
            + "Do not output code blocks unless the user explicitly asks for code.\n"
            + "Still identify the exact caller and describe the exact call-site proof in prose.\n"
        )

        which_calls_query = is_which_method_calls_question(q)

        exact_call_targets: list[str] = []
        if which_calls_query:
            for token in tokenize_query(q):
                if ("_" in token or "." in token) and len(token) >= 6:
                    for sym in self.idx.symbol_details.keys():
                        sym_tail = sym.split(".")[-1].lower()
                        if token == sym.lower() or token == sym_tail:
                            exact_call_targets.append(token)
                            break

        prioritized_callsite_snippets: list[dict] = []
        if which_calls_query and exact_call_targets:
            for sn in list(bundle.snippet_evidence):
                text = str(sn.get("text", "") or "")
                text_norm = text.lower()
                anchor = str(sn.get("anchor", "") or "").strip().lower()

                matched = False
                for target in exact_call_targets:
                    target_tail = target.split(".")[-1].lower()

                    has_direct_call = f"{target_tail}(" in text_norm and f"def {target_tail}(" not in text_norm
                    import_only = f"import {target_tail}" in text_norm and not has_direct_call
                    is_callee_definition = anchor == target_tail or anchor.endswith("." + target_tail)

                    if has_direct_call and not import_only and not is_callee_definition:
                        matched = True
                        break

                if matched:
                    prioritized_callsite_snippets.append(sn)

Step 2 — insert this exact block

        if which_calls_query and prioritized_callsite_snippets:
            def _callsite_rank(sn: dict) -> tuple:
                text = str(sn.get("text", "") or "").lower()
                anchor = str(sn.get("anchor", "") or "").strip().lower()
                line = int(sn.get("line", 10**9) or 10**9)

                call_positions = []
                import_positions = []

                for target in exact_call_targets:
                    target_tail = target.split(".")[-1].lower()

                    call_pos = text.find(f"{target_tail}(")
                    if call_pos != -1:
                        call_positions.append(call_pos)

                    import_pos = text.find(f"import {target_tail}")
                    if import_pos != -1:
                        import_positions.append(import_pos)

                first_call_pos = min(call_positions) if call_positions else 10**9
                first_import_pos = min(import_positions) if import_positions else 10**9

                has_import_before_call = first_import_pos < first_call_pos
                is_method_like_anchor = "." in anchor
                is_class_like_anchor = not is_method_like_anchor

                return (
                    1 if has_import_before_call else 0,
                    1 if is_class_like_anchor else 0,
                    first_call_pos,
                    line,
                )

            prioritized_callsite_snippets.sort(key=_callsite_rank)

Why this exact sort

It prefers:

    snippets where the first relevant evidence is a direct call, not an import

    method-level anchors like EegVisualizerLoaderLogic._select_file over class-level anchors like EegVisualizerLoaderLogic

    earlier/direct call positions inside the snippet

    earlier line numbers as a tie-breaker

That should move snippets like:

    EegVisualizerLoaderLogic._select_file

    EegVisualizerLoaderLogic._reset_visualizer

    EEGMainWindowBuilder._on_native_tab_close

above the broad class/import wrapper snippet. The debug you pasted shows exactly those better candidates already exist.

I’m checking the uploaded v10_prompt_builder.py for three things in order: syntax, duplicated definitions, and structural issues in the prompt assembly path.

I checked v10_prompt_builder.py.

Syntax: it parses successfully, so there is no immediate SyntaxError.

The bigger problems are duplicates and structure:

First, this file is structurally contaminated. It is not just a prompt-builder anymore. It contains router code like route_query_intent(...), QueryRouteDecision, locator helpers, and _norm(...) before the prompt-builder section, which belong in v10_query_router.py, not here. It also contains ProjectRetriever logic, which belongs in v10_retriever.py.

Second, you have duplicate top-level logic families in the same module. The file has one prompt-builder-style helper section with _build_answer_style_instructions(...), then also a router-style helper section with _norm, is_locator_plus_explanation_question, and route_query_intent(...) embedded in the same file. In practice, later definitions will silently override earlier ones, which makes behavior unpredictable.

Third, there is at least one clear logic bug from string concatenation: in the chain/flow prefer_code branch, the file contains the literal string "chain_evidence_rules" instead of concatenating the variable chain_evidence_rules. That means the model can literally receive the words chain_evidence_rules in the prompt instead of the rule contents.

Fourth, the file still contains temporary debug prints in production logic, including [PROMPT STYLE DEBUG] and the caller-question branch print. Those are useful while debugging, but they should not stay in the final module.

Fifth, there is a smaller duplicate/content-quality issue in _is_explain_implementation_question(...): the trigger "explain the implementation of" appears twice. It is not fatal, but it is a sign the function has been edited incrementally without cleanup.

My recommendation is:

    Split the file back into boundaries first

        keep only prompt-builder helpers + PromptBuilder in v10_prompt_builder.py

        move router pieces back to v10_query_router.py

        move retriever pieces back to v10_retriever.py

    Remove duplicate helper families

        one is_explanatory_question

        one _norm

        one query-router section, not embedded here

    Fix the real prompt bug

        replace + "chain_evidence_rules" with + chain_evidence_rules

    Remove debug prints once behavior is confirmed

So the short verdict is:

    Syntax: okay

    Duplicates: yes

    Structure: not okay; this module is currently a merged file, not a clean prompt-builder module

Structure

The biggest structural issue in this retriever is not syntax — it is intent logic and snippet policy mixed into one file.

The main things I would flag:

    runtime_first is still too broad in _retrieve_snippets(...). In the pasted file, it is set by:

runtime_first = (
    "runtime" in q
    or "probe" in q
    or is_code_localized_explanation_question(q)
)

That means a normal code-localized question can still trigger runtime snippet injection, which is not a good generic default. This is still one of the clearest structural issues in the live retriever.

    The retriever still contains a large amount of project/profile/question policy, not just retrieval mechanics. _retrieve_files(...) and _retrieve_symbols(...) are carrying a lot of intent-specific boosts for startup, topomap, reset, runtime, builder call-site logic, etc. That works, but it makes the file hard to reason about and hard to generalize.

    The current retrieve(...) in the pasted module is actually simpler than some of the debugged variants you were working on. In this pasted version, it does:

    _retrieve_files(...)

    _retrieve_symbols(...)

    _retrieve_snippets(...)

    return RetrievalBundle(...)

and does not show the later candidate-callee expansion/merge logic. So if your running app behavior depends on that later expansion work, this pasted module is not fully aligned with the live debugging path you were using.

    _retrieve_snippets(...) in the pasted file still starts with the generic symbol-evidence snippet pass and then the runtime-anchor pass. It does not show the caller-question call-site prioritization block you had been debugging with console prints. So this file snapshot is likely behind or divergent from the live code path that produced [SNIPPET CALLSITE MATCH] output.

My practical verdict

    Syntax: okay in the pasted version

    Duplicates inside this file: not the main issue

    Structure: still too overloaded; retrieval, intent policy, and project tuning are mixed together
